lets start woth phase 4 shant we?

phase 4 backbone:
Explainability and Gene Importance

The ultimate goal is not just high accuracy, but also explainable insights: Which genes drive the model’s predictions?

We’ll use two approaches:

SHAP (SHapley Additive Explanations) – to quantify feature importance for predictions
irp.nih.gov
.

Integrated Gradients (IG) for the neural network – to directly attribute importance to genes by tracing the gradient of the output w.r.t. inputs.

Additionally, because our model features are latent features (not raw genes), we’ll map the most influential latent dimensions back to genes.

5.1 SHAP on XGBoost (Latent Feature Importance)

SHAP is very efficient with tree models. We can get global importance of each latent feature for each class, and even local explanations for specific predictions.

File: notebooks/4_explainability_analysis.ipynb

import shap
# Load XGBoost model (if not in memory)
xgb_model = xgb.Booster()
xgb_model.load_model("models/xgboost_multiclass.model")

# We need shap values for multi-class. shap.TreeExplainer can handle it.
explainer = shap.TreeExplainer(xgb_model, model_output='probability')  # output = prob to get shap per class
shap_values = explainer.shap_values(X_test_lat)
# shap_values will be a list of arrays (one per class) if model_output='probability'
print(f"SHAP values type: {type(shap_values)}, length: {len(shap_values)}")
print(f"SHAP array shape for class0: {shap_values[0].shape}")  # (nsamples, n_features)


For multi-class, shap_values is a list where shap_values[i] corresponds to SHAP values for class i output. We also have explainer.expected_value which will be a list of base values for each class.

Global importance: To see which latent features matter most overall, we can take the mean absolute SHAP value per feature, averaged across all classes or separate by class.

# Mean |SHAP| for each latent feature across all test samples and classes
import numpy as np
mean_abs_shap = np.mean(np.abs(shap_values), axis=(0,2))  # average over samples and classes
# Actually, shap_values is list of [class] -> (samples x features).
# To combine classes, we can do:
shap_all_classes = np.stack(shap_values)  # shape (num_classes, nsamples, n_features)
mean_abs_shap = np.mean(np.abs(shap_all_classes), axis=(0,1))  # average over classes and samples
# Get top latent features
latent_order = np.argsort(mean_abs_shap)[::-1]
print("Latent features by importance:", latent_order[:10])


But better, we often look at class-specific importance. For example, which latent features drive AML vs ALL predictions, etc.

We can plot SHAP summary for each class:

# For class i (e.g., AML), plot summary
class_index = list(le.classes_).index("AML")  # example for AML, ensure the class name exists
shap.summary_plot(shap_values[class_index], X_test_lat, feature_names=[f"Latent{i}" for i in range(X_test_lat.shape[1])])


This will show a beeswarm plot of SHAP values for latent features for class AML output. The x-axis is impact on log-odds of that class (since we used probability, it might be log-odds or approximate contribution to probability).

We can loop through classes to generate plots:

for idx, class_name in enumerate(le.classes_):
    shap.summary_plot(shap_values[idx], X_test_lat, feature_names=[f"Latent{i}" for i in range(X_test_lat.shape[1])], show=False)
    plt.title(f"SHAP Summary for class: {class_name}")
    plt.savefig(f"reports/figures/shap_summary_{class_name}.png")
    plt.clf()


(Note: plt.clf() to clear figure each loop to avoid overlap; also ensure matplotlib.pyplot as plt and %matplotlib inline if in notebook.)

These plots show which latent features most influence each class’s prediction (red = high value of latent, blue = low value).

5.2 Map Latent Features to Genes

Now, to interpret in terms of genes, we need to know what each latent feature represents in gene space:

Since the encoder is a complex function, there’s no one-to-one mapping, but we can approximate by seeing which genes correlate with or contribute to each latent dimension.

Method: Compute the Pearson correlation between each latent feature and each gene across the TCGA training data (or use the leukemia data). The top correlated genes for latent j might indicate that latent j is capturing variation in those genes.

Alternatively, look at the decoder weights: each latent node connects to the next decoder layer (250 neurons). Tracing that back to input is messy due to multiple layers. So correlation is simpler.

Let’s do correlation using the TCGA dataset (which is large and general) to see broad patterns:

# Compute latent representations for all TCGA samples (or a subset to save time).
# If TCGA is huge, use a subset of say 2000 samples to approximate correlations.
tcga_samples = tcga_df.sample(n=2000, random_state=1)  # sample 2000 for speed
tcga_latent = encoder.predict(scaler.transform(tcga_samples.values))
# Compute Pearson correlation between each gene (in original space) and each latent dimension
gene_latent_corr = np.corrcoef(tcga_samples.values.T, tcga_latent.T)  
# This creates a big matrix of size (n_genes + n_latent) x (n_genes + n_latent). We actually want the submatrix.
gene_latent_corr = gene_latent_corr[:tcga_samples.shape[1], tcga_samples.shape[1]:]  # shape: (n_genes, n_latent)
gene_names = tcga_samples.columns
lat_names = [f"Latent{i}" for i in range(tcga_latent.shape[1])]

# Put into DataFrame for clarity
corr_df = pd.DataFrame(gene_latent_corr, index=gene_names, columns=lat_names)
corr_df.to_csv("data/processed/gene_latent_correlation.csv")
# For each latent feature, find top genes
top_genes_per_latent = {}
for lat in lat_names:
    top5 = corr_df[lat].abs().nlargest(5).index.tolist()  # top 5 by absolute correlation
    top_genes_per_latent[lat] = top5
    print(lat, "top genes:", top5)


This prints top correlated genes for each latent. Often you might see one gene with corr ~0.9 meaning that latent essentially captured that gene’s expression pattern; in other cases, a combination.

We can visualize this as a heatmap for an overview:

# Plot heatmap of correlation of top genes (say top 50 genes for each latent)
top_genes = set()
for lat, genes in top_genes_per_latent.items():
    top_genes.update(genes)
top_genes = list(top_genes)
plt.figure(figsize=(10,8))
sns.heatmap(corr_df.loc[top_genes], center=0, cmap="vlag")
plt.title("Gene-Latent Correlation (top genes)")
plt.savefig("reports/figures/gene_latent_heatmap.png")
plt.show()


This heatmap isn’t class-specific, but it shows which genes align with which latent features.

5.3 Identify Key Genes per Leukemia Subtype

Finally, combine the SHAP results and gene mappings to get genes important for each subtype:

Take the top 2-3 latent features that had high SHAP importance for class X.

For each of those latent features, list the top correlated genes.

This gives a set of candidate genes influencing that class’s prediction.

We can automate a bit:

# Suppose we want top 3 latent features for each class by mean |SHAP|
class_top_latents = {}
for idx, class_name in enumerate(le.classes_):
    # mean(|shap|) for this class across test samples
    mean_shap_class = np.mean(np.abs(shap_values[idx]), axis=0)
    top_latents_idx = np.argsort(mean_shap_class)[::-1][:3]  # indices of top 3
    class_top_latents[class_name] = top_latents_idx
    print(f"{class_name}: top latent features {top_latents_idx}")
    for j in top_latents_idx:
        genes = corr_df.iloc[:, j].abs().nlargest(5).index.tolist()
        print(f"  Latent{j} top genes: {genes}")


Now we have, for each class, a few latent features and representative genes. We can present these as final key findings. For example:

ALL: Latent7, Latent2, Latent15 are most important. Latent7 correlates with genes X, Y, Z, Latent2 with A, B, etc. These genes might be known leukemia markers (check if they make biological sense).

AML: etc.

Pitfall: Correlation doesn’t equal causation – some genes might correlate but not causally drive the classifier. However, this is a reasonable approximation for interpretability. A more direct but complex method could be computing Integrated Gradients from the MLP combined with encoder, to attribute output changes back to input genes.

5.4 Integrated Gradients for the Neural Network (Advanced, Optional)

To provide another explainability method, we attempt Integrated Gradients (IG) on the neural network model. IG will treat the entire neural pipeline (encoder + MLP) and compute the contribution of each input gene to the difference in output between a baseline and the actual input.

However, implementing IG on the full 20k-input network can be slow. We can use a single sample or a few samples as demonstration.

Approach:

Create a combined model that goes from input genes -> encoder -> MLP -> output.

Use a baseline input (e.g., all-zero after scaling, which corresponds to mean expression since we standardized data).

Compute gradients along a linear interpolation from baseline to the actual input.

Let’s do it for one sample from test:

import tensorflow.keras.backend as K

# Recreate a model from genes to output using the encoder and MLP
encoder = load_model("models/encoder.h5", compile=False)
# Freeze encoder for safety
encoder.trainable = False
# Load MLP model
mlp = load_model("models/mlp_classifier.h5", compile=False)

# Construct combined model
inp_genes = keras.Input(shape=(input_dim,), name="raw_genes")
latent_out = encoder(inp_genes)
class_out = mlp(latent_out)
full_model = keras.Model(inp_genes, class_out)

# Choose a test sample and baseline
sample_idx = 0
x = X_test_lat[sample_idx]  # latent space sample, but we need raw gene input for IG
# Actually, we need the original gene expression for that sample.
sample_id = leukemia_df.index.get_indexer([leukemia_df.index[sample_idx]])  # index of sample in original df
x_raw = leukemia_df.iloc[sample_idx].values.astype('float32')
x_raw = scaler.transform(x_raw.reshape(1, -1))  # scale the raw input
x_raw = x_raw.flatten()
baseline = np.zeros_like(x_raw)  # zero vector baseline (which corresponds to mean expression after stdscaling)


Now implement IG:

# Parameters for IG
steps = 50
# Generate interpolated inputs
interpolated_inputs = [baseline + (float(i)/steps) * (x_raw - baseline) for i in range(steps+1)]
interpolated_inputs = np.array(interpolated_inputs)

# Use GradientTape to compute gradients for each interpolated input
inp = tf.convert_to_tensor(interpolated_inputs, dtype=tf.float32)
with tf.GradientTape() as tape:
    tape.watch(inp)
    preds = full_model(inp)  # shape (steps+1, num_classes)
    # Focus on the class of interest for the sample (the true label or predicted label)
    class_idx = y_test_enc[sample_idx]
    preds_for_class = preds[:, class_idx]
# Get gradients d(pred_class)/d(input)
grads = tape.gradient(preds_for_class, inp)  # shape (steps+1, input_dim)
# Integral approximation of IG: average gradients * (input - baseline)
avg_grads = tf.reduce_mean(grads, axis=0).numpy()  # average over interpolation steps
ig_attributions = (x_raw - baseline) * avg_grads  # shape (input_dim,)
# Get top genes by absolute attribution
top_ig_genes_idx = np.argsort(np.abs(ig_attributions))[::-1][:10]
top_ig_genes = [gene_names[i] for i in top_ig_genes_idx]
print("Integrated Gradients top genes for sample:", top_ig_genes)
print("Attributions:", ig_attributions[top_ig_genes_idx])


This gives the top contributing genes for that specific sample’s prediction, according to the neural network.

We can compare these with the SHAP/latent approach results. Often there will be overlap for global important genes, but IG is local (one sample).

To get a more global view from IG, you’d repeat for multiple samples and aggregate, but that’s computationally heavy. SHAP already provided a global picture via latent features.

For brevity, we included IG mainly as a demonstration of another explainability technique.

# 4.1 - SHAP Analysis Setup
import shap
import matplotlib.pyplot as plt
import seaborn as sns
import pandas as pd
import numpy as np

print("🔍 Starting SHAP Analysis for Model Explainability...")

# Load the trained XGBoost model

xgb_model.load_model("models/xgboost_optimized.model")

# Prepare test data for SHAP

# Initialize SHAP TreeExplainer

4.2 Global Feature Importance Analysis
python
# 4.2 - Global Feature Importance
def analyze_global_shap_importance(shap_values, feature_names, class_names):
    """Analyze which latent features matter most across all predictions"""
    
    # Calculate mean absolute SHAP values for each feature
    mean_abs_shap = np.mean(np.abs(shap_values), axis=0)
    
    # Get top 15 most important latent features
    top_indices = np.argsort(mean_abs_shap)[::-1][:15]
    top_features = [feature_names[i] for i in top_indices]
    top_scores = [mean_abs_shap[i] for i in top_indices]
    
    # Plot global feature importance
    plt.figure(figsize=(10, 6))
    plt.barh(range(len(top_features)), top_scores[::-1])
    plt.yticks(range(len(top_features)), top_features[::-1])
    plt.xlabel('Mean |SHAP Value|')
    plt.title('Global Feature Importance (XGBoost)')
    plt.tight_layout()
    plt.savefig('reports/figures/global_feature_importance.png', dpi=300, bbox_inches='tight')
    plt.show()
    
    print("🔍 Top 10 Global Important Latent Features:")
    for i, (feature, score) in enumerate(zip(top_features[:10], top_scores[:10])):
        print(f"   {i+1:2d}. {feature}: {score:.4f}")
    
    return top_features, top_scores

# Run global analysis
latent_feature_names = [f"Latent_{i+1}" for i in range(30)]
top_global_features, global_scores = analyze_global_shap_importance(
    shap_values, latent_feature_names, le.classes_
)
4.3 Class-Specific SHAP Analysis
python
# 4.3 - Class-Specific Feature Importance
def analyze_class_specific_importance(shap_values, X_test, class_names, latent_names):
    """Analyze which features are important for each leukemia subtype"""
    
    # Since we have multi-class, we need to analyze each class separately
    # For XGBoost with multi:softmax, we get one set of SHAP values
    # We'll analyze by looking at samples where each class was predicted
    
    class_importance = {}
    
    for class_idx, class_name in enumerate(class_names):
        # Get samples where this class was predicted or is true
        class_mask = (y_pred_xgb == class_idx) | (y_test == class_idx)
        if np.sum(class_mask) > 0:
            class_shap = shap_values[class_mask]
            mean_abs_shap_class = np.mean(np.abs(class_shap), axis=0)
            
            # Get top features for this class
            top_indices = np.argsort(mean_abs_shap_class)[::-1][:8]
            class_importance[class_name] = {
                'features': [latent_names[i] for i in top_indices],
                'scores': [mean_abs_shap_class[i] for i in top_indices]
            }
            
            print(f"🎯 {class_name} - Top 5 Important Features:")
            for i, (feature, score) in enumerate(zip(class_importance[class_name]['features'][:5], 
                                                   class_importance[class_name]['scores'][:5])):
                print(f"   {i+1}. {feature}: {score:.4f}")
    
    return class_importance

# Run class-specific analysis
class_importance = analyze_class_specific_importance(
    shap_values, X_test, le.classes_, latent_feature_names
)
4.4 Individual Prediction Explanations
python
# 4.4 - Explain Individual Predictions
def explain_individual_predictions(explainer, X_test, sample_indices, class_names, latent_names):
    """Generate detailed explanations for specific predictions"""
    
    for idx in sample_indices:
        if idx < len(X_test):
            # Get SHAP values for this specific sample
            sample_data = X_test[idx:idx+1]
            sample_shap = explainer.shap_values(xgb.DMatrix(sample_data))
            
            # Get prediction and actual
            prediction = y_pred_xgb[idx]
            actual = y_test[idx]
            prediction_prob = xgb_model.predict(xgb.DMatrix(sample_data))[0]
            
            print(f"\n🔬 Sample {idx} Analysis:")
            print(f"   Actual: {class_names[actual]}")
            print(f"   Predicted: {class_names[prediction]}")
            print(f"   Confidence: {max(prediction_prob):.3f}")
            print(f"   Correct: {actual == prediction}")
            
            # Get top features influencing this prediction
            abs_shap = np.abs(sample_shap[0])
            top_feature_indices = np.argsort(abs_shap)[::-1][:5]
            
            print("   Top influencing features:")
            for i, feat_idx in enumerate(top_feature_indices):
                influence = sample_shap[0][feat_idx]
                direction = "increases" if influence > 0 else "decreases"
                print(f"   {i+1}. {latent_names[feat_idx]}: {influence:.4f} ({direction} {class_names[prediction]} probability)")

# Explain some interesting cases
interesting_cases = [0, 10, 25, 50]  # You can choose based on your results
explain_individual_predictions(explainer, X_test, interesting_cases, le.classes_, latent_feature_names)
4.5 Mapping Latent Features to Genes
python
# 4.5 - Gene Correlation Analysis
def map_latent_to_genes(encoder, tcga_data, gene_names, top_latents, n_genes=10):
    """Map important latent features back to original genes using correlation"""
    
    print("\n🧬 Mapping Latent Features to Genes...")
    
    # Use a subset of TCGA data for efficiency
    tcga_subset = tcga_data[:2000]  # Use first 2000 samples for speed
    
    # Get latent representations
    tcga_latent = encoder.predict(tcga_subset, verbose=0)
    
    # Calculate correlations between genes and latent features
    gene_latent_corr = np.zeros((len(gene_names), len(top_latents)))
    
    for i, latent_idx in enumerate(top_latents):
        latent_values = tcga_latent[:, latent_idx]
        for gene_idx in range(len(gene_names)):
            if gene_idx < tcga_subset.shape[1]:  # Ensure we don't exceed bounds
                gene_values = tcga_subset[:, gene_idx]
                correlation = np.corrcoef(gene_values, latent_values)[0, 1]
                gene_latent_corr[gene_idx, i] = correlation
    
    # Find top genes for each important latent feature
    latent_gene_mapping = {}
    for i, latent_idx in enumerate(top_latents):
        latent_name = f"Latent_{latent_idx+1}"
        correlations = gene_latent_corr[:, i]
        
        # Get top positive and negative correlations
        top_positive = np.argsort(correlations)[::-1][:n_genes//2]
        top_negative = np.argsort(correlations)[:n_genes//2]
        
        positive_genes = [(gene_names[idx], correlations[idx]) for idx in top_positive]
        negative_genes = [(gene_names[idx], correlations[idx]) for idx in top_negative]
        
        latent_gene_mapping[latent_name] = {
            'positive': positive_genes,
            'negative': negative_genes
        }
        
        print(f"\n🔍 {latent_name} - Top Correlated Genes:")
        print("   Positively correlated:")
        for gene, corr in positive_genes[:3]:
            print(f"     {gene}: {corr:.3f}")
        print("   Negatively correlated:")
        for gene, corr in negative_genes[:3]:
            print(f"     {gene}: {corr:.3f}")
    
    return latent_gene_mapping

# Load TCGA data for correlation analysis
def load_tcga_for_correlation():
    """Load TCGA data for gene-latent correlation analysis"""
    try:
        tcga_data = pd.read_csv("data/processed/tcga_filtered.csv", index_col=0)
        # Assuming genes are columns, samples are rows
        tcga_values = tcga_data.values.astype(np.float32)
        gene_names = tcga_data.columns.tolist()
        
        print(f"📊 Loaded TCGA data: {tcga_values.shape[0]} samples, {tcga_values.shape[1]} genes")
        return tcga_values, gene_names
    except Exception as e:
        print(f"❌ Error loading TCGA data: {e}")
        return None, None

# Load encoder for mapping
encoder = tf.keras.models.load_model("models/encoder.h5", compile=False)

# Get top global latent features for mapping
top_latent_indices = [int(feature.split('_')[1])-1 for feature in top_global_features[:5]]

# Load TCGA data and perform mapping
tcga_data, gene_names = load_tcga_for_correlation()
if tcga_data is not None:
    gene_mapping = map_latent_to_genes(encoder, tcga_data, gene_names, top_latent_indices)
else:
    print("⚠️  Skipping gene mapping - TCGA data not available")
    gene_mapping = {}
4.6 Biological Interpretation & Validation
python
# 4.6 - Biological Significance Analysis
def analyze_biological_significance(class_importance, gene_mapping):
    """Connect model explanations to known biological knowledge"""
    
    print("\n🧫 BIOLOGICAL INTERPRETATION ANALYSIS")
    print("="*50)
    
    # Known leukemia marker genes (example - you should expand this)
    known_markers = {
        'ALL': ['CD19', 'CD79A', 'PAX5', 'IKZF1'],
        'AML': ['CD33', 'CD13', 'MPO', 'NPM1'],
        'CLL': ['CD5', 'CD23', 'CD19', 'ZAP70'],
        'CML': ['BCR', 'ABL1', 'CD13', 'CD33']
    }
    
    for class_name, importance_data in class_important.items():
        print(f"\n🎯 {class_name} Analysis:")
        top_latents = importance_data['features'][:3]
        
        print(f"   Most important latent features: {', '.join(top_latents)}")
        
        # Check if we have gene mappings for these latents
        found_genes = []
        for latent in top_latents:
            if latent in gene_mapping:
                top_genes = [gene for gene, _ in gene_mapping[latent]['positive'][:3]]
                found_genes.extend(top_genes)
                print(f"   {latent} → Genes: {', '.join(top_genes)}")
        
        # Compare with known markers
        if class_name in known_markers:
            known_class_markers = known_markers[class_name]
            overlap = set(found_genes) & set(known_class_markers)
            print(f"   Known marker overlap: {len(overlap)}/{len(known_class_markers)} genes")
            if overlap:
                print(f"   Overlapping genes: {', '.join(overlap)}")

# Perform biological analysis
if gene_mapping:
    analyze_biological_significance(class_importance, gene_mapping)

# 4.7 - Comprehensive Results Visualization
def create_explainability_summary(class_importance, global_importance, gene_mapping):
    """Create summary visualizations of all explainability results"""
    
    # 1. Class-specific importance heatmap
    importance_matrix = []
    class_names = list(class_importance.keys())
    feature_names = latent_feature_names
    
    for class_name in class_names:
        class_scores = np.zeros(len(feature_names))
        for feature, score in zip(class_importance[class_name]['features'], 
                                class_importance[class_name]['scores']):
            idx = feature_names.index(feature)
            class_scores[idx] = score
        importance_matrix.append(class_scores)
    
    importance_matrix = np.array(importance_matrix)
    
    plt.figure(figsize=(12, 8))
    sns.heatmap(importance_matrix, 
                xticklabels=[f"L{i+1}" for i in range(30)],
                yticklabels=class_names,
                cmap='viridis', 
                annot=False)
    plt.title('Class-Specific Latent Feature Importance')
    plt.xlabel('Latent Features')
    plt.ylabel('Leukemia Subtypes')
    plt.tight_layout()
    plt.savefig('reports/figures/class_feature_importance_heatmap.png', dpi=300, bbox_inches='tight')
    plt.show()
    
    # 2. Create summary report
    print("\n" + "="*60)
    print("📊 EXPLAINABILITY SUMMARY REPORT")
    print("="*60)
    
    for class_name in class_names:
        top_latent = class_importance[class_name]['features'][0]
        print(f"\n{class_name}:")
        print(f"  Most important feature: {top_latent}")
        if top_latent in gene_mapping:
            top_gene = gene_mapping[top_latent]['positive'][0][0]
            print(f"  Associated gene: {top_gene}")

# Generate final summary
create_explainability_summary(class_importance, top_global_features, gene_mapping)

### 🔍 **Overall Comments**

### 🧠 **Specific Suggestions & Refinements**

#### **1. SHAP Analysis**

* Good that you’re using `TreeExplainer`; just ensure:

  ```python
  explainer = shap.TreeExplainer(xgb_model, model_output="probability")
  ```

  so that you get per-class probabilities (multi-class aware).

* When calling `explainer.shap_values(dtest)`, XGBoost returns a **list of arrays**—one per class—so your later functions (`analyze_global_shap_importance`) should expect that:

  ```python
  shap_all = np.stack(shap_values)  # (n_classes, n_samples, n_features)
  mean_abs_shap = np.mean(np.abs(shap_all), axis=(0,1))
  ```

  Then you can call your plotting function safely.

#### **2. Global Feature Importance Plot**

* Nice, but prefer horizontal bars sorted high → low (you already did `[::-1]`, great).
* Add a log-scale option (`plt.xscale("log")`) for better visibility if values differ by orders of magnitude.

#### **3. Class-Specific Analysis**

* For clarity, compute mean |SHAP| per class directly from the list:

  ```python
  for i, cls in enumerate(le.classes_):
      mean_cls = np.mean(np.abs(shap_values[i]), axis=0)
      top_idx = np.argsort(mean_cls)[::-1][:10]
      ...
  ```

  This avoids relying on `y_pred_xgb` masks (simpler, more precise).

#### **4. Gene-Latent Mapping**

* Excellent choice of Pearson correlation approach—practical and interpretable.
* Add a quick sanity check:

  ```python
  print("Correlation matrix shape:", corr_df.shape)
  assert corr_df.shape[1] == X_test_lat.shape[1]
  ```
* When plotting, `sns.clustermap` can reveal latent-gene grouping patterns more clearly than a basic heatmap.

#### **5. IG (Integrated Gradients)**

* Perfect demonstration.
  Just fix one small issue: you used

  ```python
  x = X_test_lat[sample_idx]
  ```

  but IG needs **raw gene input**, so remove that line to avoid confusion.
* For reproducibility, save IG outputs:

  ```python
  np.save("reports/attributions/ig_sample0.npy", ig_attributions)
  ```

#### **6. Reporting**

* At the end of notebook, add one markdown cell summarizing:

  * top latent → top genes → possible known leukemia markers (optional literature mapping)
  * differences in important genes between ALL vs AML vs CML
* That makes the phase report publication-ready.

# 🚨 ADD THIS CELL FIRST - Data Loading
print("📊 Loading Phase 3 data and models...")

# Load the processed latent features
latent_df = pd.read_csv("data/processed/leukemia_latent_features.csv", index_col=0)

# Apply the same class balancing as Phase 3
latent_df['disease'] = latent_df['disease'].replace({'Healthy': 'OTHER', 'UNKNOWN': 'OTHER'})

# Prepare features and labels

    X, y_encoded, test_size=0.2, stratify=y_encoded, random_state=42

    X_temp, y_temp, test_size=0.125, stratify=y_temp, random_state=42  # 0.125 = 0.1/(1-0.2)
)

🚨 SHAP FIX: Use Raw Output for Tree Path Dependent Method
python
# 4.1 - SHAP Analysis Setup (FIXED)
import shap
import matplotlib.pyplot as plt
import seaborn as sns

print("🔍 Starting SHAP Analysis for Model Explainability...")

# Prepare test data for SHAP
dtest = xgb.DMatrix(X_test, feature_names=[f"Latent_{i}" for i in range(30)])

# FIX: Use "raw" output for tree_path_dependent (default) method
explainer = shap.TreeExplainer(xgb_model, model_output="raw")
shap_values = explainer.shap_values(dtest)

print(f"✅ SHAP values computed successfully!")
print(f"   SHAP values type: {type(shap_values)}")
print(f"   SHAP values length: {len(shap_values) if isinstance(shap_values, list) else 'single array'}")

# Check the structure of SHAP values
if isinstance(shap_values, list):
    print(f"   Multi-class detected: {len(shap_values)} classes")
    for i, class_shap in enumerate(shap_values):
        print(f"   Class {i} SHAP shape: {class_shap.shape}")
else:
    print(f"   Single class SHAP shape: {shap_values.shape}")
🧠 WHY THIS FIX WORKS:
The Issue:
model_output="probability" requires interventional perturbation

But XGBoost's default is tree_path_dependent

These two are incompatible

The Solution:
Use model_output="raw" which gives margin (log-odds) values

This works with the default tree_path_dependent method

We can still interpret feature importance from raw outputs

🔧 ALTERNATIVE APPROACH (If you want probabilities):
python
# ALTERNATIVE: Use interventional method with background data
print("🔄 Using interventional method for probability outputs...")

# Use a subset of training data as background
background = X_train[:100]  # First 100 samples as background
dbackground = xgb.DMatrix(background)

explainer = shap.TreeExplainer(
    xgb_model, 
    data=dbackground,
    model_output="probability",
    feature_perturbation="interventional"
)
shap_values_prob = explainer.shap_values(dtest)

print(f"✅ Probability-based SHAP values computed!")
🎯 RECOMMENDATION: Use Raw Output
Stick with the first approach because:

Faster computation - tree_path_dependent is optimized

More stable - less sensitive to background data choice

Same feature importance - relative rankings are preserved

Industry standard - most SHAP analyses use raw outputs

📊 What "Raw" Output Means:
Raw = Margin values (log-odds before softmax)

Higher absolute SHAP values = more important features

Positive SHAP = increases prediction score for that class

Negative SHAP = decreases prediction score

🚀 Next Steps After This Fix:

print("🎯 Proceeding with global feature importance analysis...")

We are now in Phase 4: Explainability and Gene Importance.
We will use SHAP to explain the XGBoost model and the champion MLP model (if possible) and then map the important latent features back to genes.

Steps:

SHAP on XGBoost (Latent Feature Importance)

Integrated Gradients (IG) for the neural network (optional, but we can try with the champion MLP)

Map important latent dimensions back to genes by analyzing the encoder's weights.

However, note that our features are latent (30 dimensions) and not the original genes (20,034). So we need to map the latent importance to gene importance.

Approach for mapping latent to genes:

For a given latent dimension, we can look at the weights in the encoder that connect the input genes to that latent dimension.

The absolute values of these weights can indicate the importance of each gene for that latent dimension.

We can do this for the top latent features (from SHAP) and then aggregate the gene importance.

Let's start with SHAP on XGBoost.

We'll use the XGBoost model we saved (or the one in memory if available) and the test set (X_test_lat, which is the latent features of the test set).

Note: We have to be cautious with SHAP on the neural network because it can be slow. We'll focus on XGBoost for now.

Steps for SHAP on XGBoost:

a. Load the XGBoost model if not in memory.
b. Create a SHAP TreeExplainer.
c. Calculate SHAP values for the test set.
d. Analyze global and local feature importance.

Then, we'll map the important latent features to genes.

Let's code accordingly.

We'll create a new notebook for Phase 4, but for now, we'll write the code in the current notebook.

We assume that the following variables are available (from Phase 3):

X_test_lat (the test set latent features)

le (the label encoder)

xgb_model (the trained XGBoost model)

If not, we'll load them.

We'll also need the encoder model to map latent features to genes.

Let's structure the code:

Load necessary models and data.

Compute SHAP values for XGBoost.

Analyze and visualize SHAP values for each class.

Identify top latent features for each class.

Map the top latent features to genes using the encoder weights.

(Optional) Do the same for the MLP model using Integrated Gradients or SHAP (if time permits).

Let's start.

python
# 4.1 - SHAP Analysis Setup
import shap
import numpy as np
import pandas as pd
import matplotlib.pyplot as plt
import seaborn as sns
from tensorflow.keras.models import load_model
import xgboost as xgb
from sklearn.preprocessing import LabelEncoder
import warnings
warnings.filterwarnings('ignore')

print("🎯 PHASE 4: Model Explainability Analysis")
print("=" * 50)
python
# 4.2 - Load Models and Data
# Load champion model
champion_model = load_model("models/final_champion_model.h5")
print("✅ Champion MLP model loaded")

# Load XGBoost model for comparison
xgb_model = xgb.Booster()
xgb_model.load_model("models/xgboost_optimized.model")
print("✅ XGBoost model loaded")

# Load latent features and labels
latent_df = pd.read_csv("data/processed/leukemia_latent_features.csv", index_col=0)
print("✅ Latent features loaded")

# Load test data from Phase 3
final_results = pd.read_csv("data/processed/final_predictions.csv")
X_test = latent_df.iloc[final_results.index[:72], :30].values  # Test set indices
y_test = final_results['true'].values

# Label encoder
le = LabelEncoder()
le.fit(['ALL', 'AML', 'CLL', 'CML', 'OTHER'])

print(f"📊 Test set: {X_test.shape[0]} samples, {X_test.shape[1]} features")
python
# 4.3 - SHAP Analysis for XGBoost
print("🔍 Running SHAP Analysis for XGBoost...")

# Create SHAP explainer for XGBoost
explainer_xgb = shap.TreeExplainer(xgb_model)
shap_values_xgb = explainer_xgb.shap_values(X_test)

# Convert to numpy array for multi-class handling
shap_values_array = np.array(shap_values_xgb)
print(f"📈 SHAP values shape: {shap_values_array.shape}")

# Calculate global feature importance
global_shap_importance = np.mean(np.abs(shap_values_array), axis=(0, 1))
top_latent_features = np.argsort(global_shap_importance)[::-1][:10]

print("🔝 Top 10 Most Important Latent Features (XGBoost):")
for i, feature_idx in enumerate(top_latent_features):
    print(f"   {i+1:2d}. Latent_Dim_{feature_idx+1}: {global_shap_importance[feature_idx]:.4f}")
python
# 4.4 - SHAP Visualization for XGBoost
# Create feature names
feature_names = [f'Latent_Dim_{i+1}' for i in range(30)]

# Summary plot
plt.figure(figsize=(10, 8))
shap.summary_plot(shap_values_array, X_test, feature_names=feature_names, 
                  class_names=le.classes_, show=False)
plt.title("SHAP Summary Plot - XGBoost Model", fontsize=14, fontweight='bold')
plt.tight_layout()
plt.savefig('reports/figures/shap_summary_xgboost.png', dpi=300, bbox_inches='tight')
plt.show()

# Bar plot for global importance
plt.figure(figsize=(12, 6))
shap.summary_plot(shap_values_array, X_test, feature_names=feature_names, 
                  plot_type="bar", class_names=le.classes_, show=False)
plt.title("SHAP Feature Importance - XGBoost Model", fontsize=14, fontweight='bold')
plt.tight_layout()
plt.savefig('reports/figures/shap_bar_xgboost.png', dpi=300, bbox_inches='tight')
plt.show()
python
# 4.5 - Class-Specific SHAP Analysis
print("🎯 Class-Specific Feature Importance")

fig, axes = plt.subplots(2, 3, figsize=(18, 12))
axes = axes.ravel()

for i, class_name in enumerate(le.classes_):
    plt.sca(axes[i])
    shap.summary_plot(shap_values_array[i], X_test, feature_names=feature_names, 
                      show=False, max_display=10)
    plt.title(f'Class: {class_name}', fontweight='bold')
    plt.xlabel(f'SHAP value impact on {class_name} prediction')

# Remove empty subplot
axes[-1].set_visible(False)

plt.tight_layout()
plt.savefig('reports/figures/shap_class_breakdown.png', dpi=300, bbox_inches='tight')
plt.show()
python
# 4.6 - Individual Prediction Explanations
print("🔬 Individual Prediction Explanations")

# Get some interesting cases - correct and misclassified
sample_indices = []
for class_name in le.classes_:
    class_mask = final_results['true'] == class_name
    if class_mask.sum() > 0:
        sample_indices.append(final_results[class_mask].index[0])

print("📝 Sample predictions to explain:")
for idx in sample_indices[:3]:  # Show first 3
    true_label = final_results.loc[idx, 'true']
    pred_label = final_results.loc[idx, 'mlp_v2_pred']
    print(f"   Sample {idx}: True={true_label}, Predicted={pred_label}")
    
    # SHAP force plot for this sample
    plt.figure(figsize=(10, 3))
    class_idx = list(le.classes_).index(pred_label)
    shap.force_plot(explainer_xgb.expected_value[class_idx], 
                   shap_values_array[class_idx][idx], 
                   X_test[idx], 
                   feature_names=feature_names,
                   matplotlib=True, show=False)
    plt.title(f"SHAP Explanation - Sample {idx}\nTrue: {true_label} → Pred: {pred_label}")
    plt.tight_layout()
    plt.savefig(f'reports/figures/shap_force_sample_{idx}.png', dpi=300, bbox_inches='tight')
    plt.show()
python
# 4.7 - Latent Space to Gene Mapping
print("🧬 Mapping Latent Features to Original Genes")

# Load encoder and original data
encoder = load_model("models/encoder.h5")
leukemia_data = pd.read_csv("data/processed/leukemia_filtered.csv", index_col=0)
print("✅ Encoder and original data loaded")

# Get encoder weights for the first layer (genes → latent space)
encoder_weights = encoder.layers[1].get_weights()[0]  # Dense layer weights
print(f"📊 Encoder weight matrix: {encoder_weights.shape}")

# Analyze which genes contribute most to important latent dimensions
top_latent_dims = top_latent_features[:5]  # Top 5 latent dimensions

print("🔝 Top Genes for Most Important Latent Dimensions:")
for latent_dim in top_latent_dims:
    # Get weights for this latent dimension
    gene_weights = encoder_weights[:, latent_dim]
    top_gene_indices = np.argsort(np.abs(gene_weights))[::-1][:10]
    
    print(f"\n🎯 Latent Dimension {latent_dim + 1}:")
    for i, gene_idx in enumerate(top_gene_indices):
        weight = gene_weights[gene_idx]
        print(f"   {i+1:2d}. Gene_{gene_idx+1}: {weight:.4f}")
python
# 4.8 - Biological Interpretation
print("🔍 Biological Relevance Analysis")

# Create gene importance DataFrame
gene_importance_data = []

for latent_dim in top_latent_dims:
    gene_weights = encoder_weights[:, latent_dim]
    top_gene_indices = np.argsort(np.abs(gene_weights))[::-1][:20]
    
    for gene_idx in top_gene_indices:
        gene_importance_data.append({
            'latent_dimension': f'Latent_{latent_dim + 1}',
            'gene_index': gene_idx,
            'absolute_weight': abs(gene_weights[gene_idx]),
            'weight': gene_weights[gene_idx]
        })

gene_importance_df = pd.DataFrame(gene_importance_data)

# Plot top genes across latent dimensions
plt.figure(figsize=(14, 8))
top_genes_overall = (gene_importance_df.groupby('gene_index')['absolute_weight']
                    .mean()
                    .sort_values(ascending=False)
                    .head(20))

plt.barh(range(len(top_genes_overall)), top_genes_overall.values[::-1])
plt.yticks(range(len(top_genes_overall)), [f'Gene_{idx}' for idx in top_genes_overall.index[::-1]])
plt.xlabel('Average Absolute Weight Across Top Latent Dimensions')
plt.title('Top 20 Most Biologically Important Genes', fontweight='bold')
plt.tight_layout()
plt.savefig('reports/figures/top_biological_genes.png', dpi=300, bbox_inches='tight')
plt.show()

print("💾 Saving gene importance analysis...")
gene_importance_df.to_csv("data/processed/gene_importance_analysis.csv", index=False)

# 4.10 - Clinical Relevance Summary
print("🏥 Clinical Relevance Assessment")

# Calculate confidence scores for predictions
y_pred_proba = champion_model.predict(X_test)
confidence_scores = np.max(y_pred_proba, axis=1)

# Analyze misclassifications
misclassified = final_results[final_results['true'] != final_results['mlp_v2_pred']]
correctly_classified = final_results[final_results['true'] == final_results['mlp_v2_pred']]

print("📈 Confidence Analysis:")
print(f"   Average Confidence (Correct): {np.mean(confidence_scores[correctly_classified.index]):.3f}")
print(f"   Average Confidence (Wrong): {np.mean(confidence_scores[misclassified.index]):.3f}")
print(f"   Overall Accuracy: {len(correctly_classified)/len(final_results):.3f}")

# Confidence distribution
plt.figure(figsize=(10, 6))
plt.hist(confidence_scores[correctly_classified.index], alpha=0.7, label='Correct', bins=20)
plt.hist(confidence_scores[misclassified.index], alpha=0.7, label='Misclassified', bins=20)
plt.xlabel('Prediction Confidence')
plt.ylabel('Frequency')
plt.title('Prediction Confidence Distribution')

# 4.4 – SHAP Visualization for XGBoost (RECOMPUTE + FIXED)
import shap
import matplotlib.pyplot as plt
import numpy as np

# Make sure the explainer uses the trained model directly
explainer_xgb = shap.TreeExplainer(best_xgb)
shap_values_xgb = explainer_xgb.shap_values(X_test)

# Handle both binary and multiclass cases automatically
if isinstance(shap_values_xgb, list):
    # Multi-class → shap_values_xgb is a list of arrays (n_classes)
    for i, class_name in enumerate(le.classes_):
        print(f"📊 Generating SHAP summary for class: {class_name}")

        shap.summary_plot(shap_values_xgb[i], X_test,

        plt.savefig(f"reports/figures/shap_summary_xgboost_{class_name}.png",
                    dpi=300, bbox_inches="tight")
        plt.show()

    print("📊 Generating mean SHAP bar plot across classes...")
    mean_shap_values = np.mean(np.abs(np.array(shap_values_xgb)), axis=0)
    plt.figure(figsize=(12, 6))
    shap.summary_plot(mean_shap_values, X_test,
                      feature_names=feature_names, plot_type="bar", show=False)
    plt.title("Average SHAP Feature Importance – XGBoost", fontsize=14, fontweight="bold")
    plt.tight_layout()

    print("📊 Binary classifier – generating single SHAP summary...")

    shap.summary_plot(shap_values_xgb, X_test,

import shap

    # Multi-class → shap_values_xgb is a list of arrays (n_classes)
    for i, class_name in enumerate(le.classes_):
        print(f"📊 Generating SHAP summary for class: {class_name}")

        plt.savefig(f"reports/figures/shap_summary_xgboost_{class_name}.png",
                    dpi=300, bbox_inches="tight")
        plt.show()

    # Global importance across classes
    print("📊 Generating mean SHAP bar plot across classes...")
    mean_shap_values = np.mean(np.abs(np.array(shap_values_xgb)), axis=0)
    plt.figure(figsize=(12, 6))
    shap.summary_plot(mean_shap_values, X_test,
                      feature_names=feature_names, plot_type="bar", show=False)
    plt.title("Average SHAP Feature Importance – XGBoost", fontsize=14, fontweight="bold")
    plt.tight_layout()
    plt.savefig("reports/figures/shap_bar_xgboost_mean.png", dpi=300, bbox_inches="tight")
    plt.show()

else:
    # Single-class (binary) case
    print("📊 Binary classifier – generating single SHAP summary...")
    plt.figure(figsize=(10, 8))
    shap.summary_plot(shap_values_xgb, X_test,
                      feature_names=feature_names, show=False)
    plt.title("SHAP Summary Plot – XGBoost", fontsize=14, fontweight="bold")
    plt.tight_layout()
    plt.savefig("reports/figures/shap_summary_xgboost.png", dpi=300, bbox_inches="tight")
    plt.show()

    plt.figure(figsize=(12, 6))
    shap.summary_plot(shap_values_xgb, X_test,
                      feature_names=feature_names, plot_type="bar", show=False)
    plt.title("SHAP Feature Importance – XGBoost", fontsize=14, fontweight="bold")
    plt.tight_layout()
    plt.savefig("reports/figures/shap_bar_xgboost.png", dpi=300, bbox_inches="tight")
    plt.show()

We are now in Phase 4: Explainability and Gene Importance.
We will use SHAP to explain the XGBoost model and the champion MLP model (if possible) and then map the important latent features back to genes.

Steps:

SHAP on XGBoost (Latent Feature Importance)

Integrated Gradients (IG) for the neural network (optional, but we can try with the champion MLP)

Map important latent dimensions back to genes by analyzing the encoder's weights.

However, note that our features are latent (30 dimensions) and not the original genes (20,034). So we need to map the latent importance to gene importance.

Approach for mapping latent to genes:

For a given latent dimension, we can look at the weights in the encoder that connect the input genes to that latent dimension.

The absolute values of these weights can indicate the importance of each gene for that latent dimension.

We can do this for the top latent features (from SHAP) and then aggregate the gene importance.

Let's start with SHAP on XGBoost.

We'll use the XGBoost model we saved (or the one in memory if available) and the test set (X_test_lat, which is the latent features of the test set).

Note: We have to be cautious with SHAP on the neural network because it can be slow. We'll focus on XGBoost for now.

Steps for SHAP on XGBoost:

a. Load the XGBoost model if not in memory.
b. Create a SHAP TreeExplainer.
c. Calculate SHAP values for the test set.
d. Analyze global and local feature importance.

Then, we'll map the important latent features to genes.

Let's code accordingly.

We'll create a new notebook for Phase 4, but for now, we'll write the code in the current notebook.

We assume that the following variables are available (from Phase 3):

X_test_lat (the test set latent features)

le (the label encoder)

xgb_model (the trained XGBoost model)

If not, we'll load them.

We'll also need the encoder model to map latent features to genes.

Let's structure the code:

Load necessary models and data.

Compute SHAP values for XGBoost.

Analyze and visualize SHAP values for each class.

Identify top latent features for each class.

Map the top latent features to genes using the encoder weights.

(Optional) Do the same for the MLP model using Integrated Gradients or SHAP (if time permits).

Let's start.

Here are all the updated Phase 4 cells ready for copy-paste:

python
# 4.1 - SHAP Analysis Setup
import shap
import numpy as np
import pandas as pd
import matplotlib.pyplot as plt
import seaborn as sns
from tensorflow.keras.models import load_model
import xgboost as xgb
from sklearn.preprocessing import LabelEncoder
import warnings
warnings.filterwarnings('ignore')

print("🎯 PHASE 4: Model Explainability Analysis")
print("=" * 50)
python
# 4.2 - Load Models and Data
# Load champion model
champion_model = load_model("models/final_champion_model.h5")
print("✅ Champion MLP model loaded")

# Load XGBoost model for comparison
xgb_model = xgb.Booster()
xgb_model.load_model("models/xgboost_optimized.model")
print("✅ XGBoost model loaded")

# Load latent features and labels
latent_df = pd.read_csv("data/processed/leukemia_latent_features.csv", index_col=0)
print("✅ Latent features loaded")

# Load test data from Phase 3
final_results = pd.read_csv("data/processed/final_predictions.csv")
X_test = latent_df.iloc[final_results.index[:72], :30].values  # Test set indices
y_test = final_results['true'].values

# Label encoder
le = LabelEncoder()
le.fit(['ALL', 'AML', 'CLL', 'CML', 'OTHER'])

print(f"📊 Test set: {X_test.shape[0]} samples, {X_test.shape[1]} features")
python
# 4.3 - SHAP Analysis for XGBoost
print("🔍 Running SHAP Analysis for XGBoost...")

# Create SHAP explainer for XGBoost
explainer_xgb = shap.TreeExplainer(xgb_model)
shap_values_xgb = explainer_xgb.shap_values(X_test)

# Convert to numpy array for multi-class handling
shap_values_array = np.array(shap_values_xgb)
print(f"📈 SHAP values shape: {shap_values_array.shape}")

# Calculate global feature importance
global_shap_importance = np.mean(np.abs(shap_values_array), axis=(0, 1))
top_latent_features = np.argsort(global_shap_importance)[::-1][:10]

print("🔝 Top 10 Most Important Latent Features (XGBoost):")
for i, feature_idx in enumerate(top_latent_features):
    print(f"   {i+1:2d}. Latent_Dim_{feature_idx+1}: {global_shap_importance[feature_idx]:.4f}")
python
# 4.4 - SHAP Visualization for XGBoost
# Create feature names
feature_names = [f'Latent_Dim_{i+1}' for i in range(30)]

# Summary plot
plt.figure(figsize=(10, 8))
shap.summary_plot(shap_values_array, X_test, feature_names=feature_names, 
                  class_names=le.classes_, show=False)
plt.title("SHAP Summary Plot - XGBoost Model", fontsize=14, fontweight='bold')
plt.tight_layout()
plt.savefig('reports/figures/shap_summary_xgboost.png', dpi=300, bbox_inches='tight')
plt.show()

# Bar plot for global importance
plt.figure(figsize=(12, 6))
shap.summary_plot(shap_values_array, X_test, feature_names=feature_names, 
                  plot_type="bar", class_names=le.classes_, show=False)
plt.title("SHAP Feature Importance - XGBoost Model", fontsize=14, fontweight='bold')
plt.tight_layout()
plt.savefig('reports/figures/shap_bar_xgboost.png', dpi=300, bbox_inches='tight')
plt.show()
python
# 4.5 - Class-Specific SHAP Analysis
print("🎯 Class-Specific Feature Importance")

fig, axes = plt.subplots(2, 3, figsize=(18, 12))
axes = axes.ravel()

for i, class_name in enumerate(le.classes_):
    plt.sca(axes[i])
    shap.summary_plot(shap_values_array[i], X_test, feature_names=feature_names, 
                      show=False, max_display=10)
    plt.title(f'Class: {class_name}', fontweight='bold')
    plt.xlabel(f'SHAP value impact on {class_name} prediction')

# Remove empty subplot
axes[-1].set_visible(False)

plt.tight_layout()
plt.savefig('reports/figures/shap_class_breakdown.png', dpi=300, bbox_inches='tight')
plt.show()
python
# 4.6 - Individual Prediction Explanations
print("🔬 Individual Prediction Explanations")

# Get some interesting cases - correct and misclassified
sample_indices = []
for class_name in le.classes_:
    class_mask = final_results['true'] == class_name
    if class_mask.sum() > 0:
        sample_indices.append(final_results[class_mask].index[0])

print("📝 Sample predictions to explain:")
for idx in sample_indices[:3]:  # Show first 3
    true_label = final_results.loc[idx, 'true']
    pred_label = final_results.loc[idx, 'mlp_v2_pred']
    print(f"   Sample {idx}: True={true_label}, Predicted={pred_label}")
    
    # SHAP force plot for this sample
    plt.figure(figsize=(10, 3))
    class_idx = list(le.classes_).index(pred_label)
    shap.force_plot(explainer_xgb.expected_value[class_idx], 
                   shap_values_array[class_idx][idx], 
                   X_test[idx], 
                   feature_names=feature_names,
                   matplotlib=True, show=False)
    plt.title(f"SHAP Explanation - Sample {idx}\nTrue: {true_label} → Pred: {pred_label}")
    plt.tight_layout()
    plt.savefig(f'reports/figures/shap_force_sample_{idx}.png', dpi=300, bbox_inches='tight')
    plt.show()
python
# 4.7 - Latent Space to Gene Mapping
print("🧬 Mapping Latent Features to Original Genes")

# Load encoder and original data
encoder = load_model("models/encoder.h5")
leukemia_data = pd.read_csv("data/processed/leukemia_filtered.csv", index_col=0)
print("✅ Encoder and original data loaded")

# Get encoder weights for the first layer (genes → latent space)
encoder_weights = encoder.layers[1].get_weights()[0]  # Dense layer weights
print(f"📊 Encoder weight matrix: {encoder_weights.shape}")

# Analyze which genes contribute most to important latent dimensions
top_latent_dims = top_latent_features[:5]  # Top 5 latent dimensions

print("🔝 Top Genes for Most Important Latent Dimensions:")
for latent_dim in top_latent_dims:
    # Get weights for this latent dimension
    gene_weights = encoder_weights[:, latent_dim]
    top_gene_indices = np.argsort(np.abs(gene_weights))[::-1][:10]
    
    print(f"\n🎯 Latent Dimension {latent_dim + 1}:")
    for i, gene_idx in enumerate(top_gene_indices):
        weight = gene_weights[gene_idx]
        print(f"   {i+1:2d}. Gene_{gene_idx+1}: {weight:.4f}")
python
# 4.8 - Biological Interpretation
print("🔍 Biological Relevance Analysis")

# Create gene importance DataFrame
gene_importance_data = []

for latent_dim in top_latent_dims:
    gene_weights = encoder_weights[:, latent_dim]
    top_gene_indices = np.argsort(np.abs(gene_weights))[::-1][:20]
    
    for gene_idx in top_gene_indices:
        gene_importance_data.append({
            'latent_dimension': f'Latent_{latent_dim + 1}',
            'gene_index': gene_idx,
            'absolute_weight': abs(gene_weights[gene_idx]),
            'weight': gene_weights[gene_idx]
        })

gene_importance_df = pd.DataFrame(gene_importance_data)

# Plot top genes across latent dimensions
plt.figure(figsize=(14, 8))
top_genes_overall = (gene_importance_df.groupby('gene_index')['absolute_weight']
                    .mean()
                    .sort_values(ascending=False)
                    .head(20))

plt.barh(range(len(top_genes_overall)), top_genes_overall.values[::-1])
plt.yticks(range(len(top_genes_overall)), [f'Gene_{idx}' for idx in top_genes_overall.index[::-1]])
plt.xlabel('Average Absolute Weight Across Top Latent Dimensions')
plt.title('Top 20 Most Biologically Important Genes', fontweight='bold')
plt.tight_layout()
plt.savefig('reports/figures/top_biological_genes.png', dpi=300, bbox_inches='tight')
plt.show()

print("💾 Saving gene importance analysis...")
gene_importance_df.to_csv("data/processed/gene_importance_analysis.csv", index=False)
python
# 4.9 - Model Comparison Explainability
print("📊 Comparing XGBoost vs MLP Feature Importance")

# For MLP, we can use permutation importance or integrated gradients
# Let's use a simpler approach: analyze the first layer weights of MLP

mlp_weights = champion_model.layers[1].get_weights()[0]  # First hidden layer
mlp_feature_importance = np.mean(np.abs(mlp_weights), axis=1)
top_mlp_features = np.argsort(mlp_feature_importance)[::-1][:10]

print("🔝 Top 10 Features - MLP vs XGBoost:")
print("   Rank |   MLP Importance   |  XGBoost Importance | Feature")
print("   " + "-" * 55)

for i in range(10):
    mlp_rank = np.where(top_mlp_features == i)[0]
    xgb_rank = np.where(top_latent_features == i)[0]
    
    mlp_str = f"#{mlp_rank[0]+1}" if len(mlp_rank) > 0 else " - "
    xgb_str = f"#{xgb_rank[0]+1}" if len(xgb_rank) > 0 else " - "
    
    print(f"   {i+1:2d}   | {mlp_str:>16} | {xgb_str:>18} | Latent_Dim_{i+1}")

# Visual comparison
plt.figure(figsize=(12, 6))
plt.subplot(1, 2, 1)
plt.barh(range(10), mlp_feature_importance[top_mlp_features][::-1])
plt.yticks(range(10), [f'Latent_{i+1}' for i in top_mlp_features[::-1]])
plt.xlabel('Mean Absolute Weight')
plt.title('MLP Feature Importance')

plt.subplot(1, 2, 2)
plt.barh(range(10), global_shap_importance[top_latent_features][::-1])
plt.yticks(range(10), [f'Latent_{i+1}' for i in top_latent_features[::-1]])
plt.xlabel('Mean |SHAP| Value')
plt.title('XGBoost Feature Importance')

plt.tight_layout()
plt.savefig('reports/figures/model_comparison_importance.png', dpi=300, bbox_inches='tight')
plt.show()
python
# 4.10 - Clinical Relevance Summary
print("🏥 Clinical Relevance Assessment")

# Calculate confidence scores for predictions
y_pred_proba = champion_model.predict(X_test)
confidence_scores = np.max(y_pred_proba, axis=1)

# Analyze misclassifications
misclassified = final_results[final_results['true'] != final_results['mlp_v2_pred']]
correctly_classified = final_results[final_results['true'] == final_results['mlp_v2_pred']]

print("📈 Confidence Analysis:")
print(f"   Average Confidence (Correct): {np.mean(confidence_scores[correctly_classified.index]):.3f}")
print(f"   Average Confidence (Wrong): {np.mean(confidence_scores[misclassified.index]):.3f}")
print(f"   Overall Accuracy: {len(correctly_classified)/len(final_results):.3f}")

# Confidence distribution
plt.figure(figsize=(10, 6))
plt.hist(confidence_scores[correctly_classified.index], alpha=0.7, label='Correct', bins=20)
plt.hist(confidence_scores[misclassified.index], alpha=0.7, label='Misclassified', bins=20)
plt.xlabel('Prediction Confidence')
plt.ylabel('Frequency')
plt.title('Prediction Confidence Distribution')
plt.legend()
plt.grid(True, alpha=0.3)
plt.tight_layout()
plt.savefig('reports/figures/confidence_distribution.png', dpi=300, bbox_inches='tight')
plt.show()

print("✅ Phase 4 Explainability Analysis Complete!")
print("🎯 Ready for Clinical Deployment & Further Validation")

# 4.4 - SHAP Visualization for XGBoost (CORRECTED)
print("🔄 Running corrected SHAP visualization...")

# Create feature names
feature_names = [f'Latent_Dim_{i+1}' for i in range(30)]

if isinstance(shap_values_xgb, list):
    # Convert list to numpy array for multi-class
    shap_array = np.array(shap_values_xgb)
    # For summary plot, we need to use the first class or mean across classes
    shap_for_plot = shap_array[0]  # Use first class for visualization
else:
    shap_for_plot = shap_values_xgb

This handles both list and array formats of SHAP values that can occur with multi-class models.

How did you compute shap_values_xgb — was it from this line?

shap_values_xgb = explainer_xgb.shap_values(X_test)
Yes exactly! The issue is that shap_values_xgb from explainer_xgb.shap_values(X_test) returns a list of arrays for multi-class, but SHAP's plotting functions expect a single array.

Here's the definitive fix:

python
# 4.4 - SHAP Visualization for XGBoost (DEFINITIVE FIX)
print("🔄 Running definitive SHAP visualization...")

# Create feature names
feature_names = [f'Latent_Dim_{i+1}' for i in range(30)]

# FIX: Properly handle multi-class SHAP output
# shap_values_xgb is a LIST of arrays - one per class
print(f"SHAP values type: {type(shap_values_xgb)}")
print(f"SHAP values length: {len(shap_values_xgb)}")
print(f"First element shape: {shap_values_xgb[0].shape}")

# For global summary, we can average absolute SHAP values across classes
shap_combined = np.mean(np.abs(shap_values_xgb), axis=0)

plt.figure(figsize=(10, 8))
shap.summary_plot(shap_combined, X_test, feature_names=feature_names, show=False)
plt.title("SHAP Summary Plot - XGBoost Model (Combined Classes)", fontsize=14, fontweight='bold')
plt.tight_layout()
plt.savefig('reports/figures/shap_summary_xgboost.png', dpi=300, bbox_inches='tight')
plt.show()

# Bar plot for global importance
plt.figure(figsize=(12, 6))
shap.summary_plot(shap_combined, X_test, feature_names=feature_names, plot_type="bar", show=False)
plt.title("SHAP Feature Importance - XGBoost Model", fontsize=14, fontweight='bold')
plt.tight_layout()
plt.savefig('reports/figures/shap_bar_xgboost.png', dpi=300, bbox_inches='tight')
plt.show()
This combines SHAP values across all classes for a global feature importance view.

# 4.4 – SHAP Visualization for XGBoost (RECOMPUTE + FIXED)
import shap
import matplotlib.pyplot as plt
import numpy as np

print("🔄 Recomputing SHAP values for XGBoost...")

# Make sure the explainer uses the trained model directly
explainer_xgb = shap.TreeExplainer(best_xgb)
shap_values_xgb = explainer_xgb.shap_values(X_test)

print("✅ New shap_values_xgb shape:", np.array(shap_values_xgb).shape)
feature_names = [f"Latent_Dim_{i+1}" for i in range(X_test.shape[1])]

# Handle both binary and multiclass cases automatically
if isinstance(shap_values_xgb, list):
    # Multi-class → shap_values_xgb is a list of arrays (n_classes)
    for i, class_name in enumerate(le.classes_):
        print(f"📊 Generating SHAP summary for class: {class_name}")
        plt.figure(figsize=(10, 8))
        shap.summary_plot(shap_values_xgb[i], X_test,
                          feature_names=feature_names, show=False)
        plt.title(f"SHAP Summary Plot – XGBoost ({class_name})", fontsize=14, fontweight="bold")
        plt.tight_layout()
        plt.savefig(f"reports/figures/shap_summary_xgboost_{class_name}.png",
                    dpi=300, bbox_inches="tight")
        plt.show()

    # Global importance across classes
    print("📊 Generating mean SHAP bar plot across classes...")
    mean_shap_values = np.mean(np.abs(np.array(shap_values_xgb)), axis=0)
    plt.figure(figsize=(12, 6))
    shap.summary_plot(mean_shap_values, X_test,
                      feature_names=feature_names, plot_type="bar", show=False)
    plt.title("Average SHAP Feature Importance – XGBoost", fontsize=14, fontweight="bold")
    plt.tight_layout()
    plt.savefig("reports/figures/shap_bar_xgboost_mean.png", dpi=300, bbox_inches="tight")
    plt.show()

else:
    # Single-class (binary) case
    print("📊 Binary classifier – generating single SHAP summary...")
    plt.figure(figsize=(10, 8))
    shap.summary_plot(shap_values_xgb, X_test,
                      feature_names=feature_names, show=False)
    plt.title("SHAP Summary Plot – XGBoost", fontsize=14, fontweight="bold")
    plt.tight_layout()
    plt.savefig("reports/figures/shap_summary_xgboost.png", dpi=300, bbox_inches="tight")
    plt.show()

    plt.figure(figsize=(12, 6))
    shap.summary_plot(shap_values_xgb, X_test,
                      feature_names=feature_names, plot_type="bar", show=False)
    plt.title("SHAP Feature Importance – XGBoost", fontsize=14, fontweight="bold")
    plt.tight_layout()
    plt.savefig("reports/figures/shap_bar_xgboost.png", dpi=300, bbox_inches="tight")
    plt.show()
